# External Agents for Entire CLI

This repository contains standalone external agent binaries that extend the [Entire CLI](https://github.com/entireio/cli) with support for additional AI coding agents.

## What Are External Agents?

External agents are standalone binaries (named `entire-agent-<name>`) that teach Entire CLI how to work with AI coding agents it doesn't natively support. When an external agent is installed on your `PATH`, Entire discovers it automatically and gains the ability to:

- **Create checkpoints** during AI coding sessions so you can rewind mistakes
- **Capture transcripts** of what the AI agent did and why
- **Install hooks** so the AI agent's lifecycle events (start, stop, commit) flow through Entire

External agents communicate with Entire CLI via subcommands that accept and return JSON over stdin/stdout. See the [external agent protocol spec](https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/docs/architecture/external-agent-protocol.md) for the full interface.

## Available Agents

| Agent | Directory | Status |
|-------|-----------|--------|
| [Kiro](agents/entire-agent-kiro/) | `agents/entire-agent-kiro/` | Implemented — hooks + transcript analysis |
| [Amp](agents/entire-agent-amp/) | `agents/entire-agent-amp/` | Implemented — hooks + transcript analysis + token calculation + compact transcripts |

See each agent's own README for setup and usage instructions.

## Enabling External Agents

External agent discovery is opt-in. Once an `entire-agent-<name>` binary is on your `PATH`, set `external_agents: true` in the repo's `.entire/settings.json` so Entire scans for it:

```json
{
  "external_agents": true
}
```

Without this flag, Entire ignores external agent binaries even when they're installed.

## Building a New External Agent

This repo includes a skill that guides you through building a new external agent with two test layers:

1. **Protocol compliance** — generic subcommand coverage from [`entireio/external-agents-tests`](https://github.com/entireio/external-agents-tests)
2. **Lifecycle integration** — repo-local `e2e/` tests that exercise `entire enable`, prompt execution, checkpoints, and rewind
3. **Implementation** — build the binary until both layers pass, then add unit tests

### Getting Started — Zero Setup

Clone the repo and open it in your AI coding tool. Each tool auto-discovers the skill with no additional configuration:

| Tool | How it discovers | What to say |
|------|-----------------|-------------|
| **Claude Code** | `.claude/skills/` directory | `/entire-external-agent` |
| **Codex** | `AGENTS.md` at project root | "Build an external agent" |
| **Cursor** | `.cursor/rules/` directory | "Build an external agent" |
| **OpenCode** | `.opencode/plugins/` auto-loaded | "Build an external agent" |

The skill files live in `.claude/skills/entire-external-agent/` if you want to read the details.

## Testing

Testing is intentionally split:

- **Generic protocol checks** run in GitHub Actions via [`entireio/external-agents-tests`](https://github.com/entireio/external-agents-tests). The workflow builds each `entire-agent-*` binary in this repo and runs the shared compliance suite against it.
- **Agent unit/build checks** run in GitHub Actions for every `agents/entire-agent-*` directory discovered at runtime.
- **Lifecycle tests** stay in this repo's [`e2e/`](e2e/) harness. These verify the parts that depend on Entire itself and on the real agent CLI: prompt execution, hook installation after `entire enable`, checkpoint creation, rewind behavior, and interactive sessions.
- **Unit tests** live with each agent implementation under [`agents/`](agents/).

### Running Tests

```bash
# Run default test suite for all agents
mise run test

# Same as test, kept for explicit CI-style naming
mise run test:ci

# Run lifecycle integration tests from this repo
mise run test:e2e

# Same as test:e2e, kept as the explicit name
mise run test:e2e:lifecycle
```

Protocol compliance runs in CI through [`.github/workflows/protocol-compliance.yml`](.github/workflows/protocol-compliance.yml).

For a newly added agent to be picked up automatically by local root tasks and CI, add `agents/entire-agent-<name>/mise.toml` with `build` and `test` tasks. The shared runner falls back to Go defaults when `go.mod` exists, but the `mise` tasks are the contract for non-standard setups.

### Lifecycle Harness Architecture

The lifecycle harness auto-discovers and builds all agents in `agents/` via `TestMain`:

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `e2e/setup_test.go` | `TestMain` entry point — discovers agents, builds binaries, configures PATH |
| `e2e/lifecycle_test.go` | Shared lifecycle scenarios run against every registered agent |
| `e2e/agents/` | Agent adapters for the real CLIs used during lifecycle tests |
| `e2e/entire/` | Entire CLI wrappers used by lifecycle assertions |
| `e2e/testutil/` | Repo setup, artifact capture, git helpers, and checkpoint assertions |

### Environment Variables

| Variable | Description |
```

## Plan Mode Override

If plan mode is active when this skill is invoked, call ExitPlanMode immediately
before doing anything else. This skill has its own multi-phase procedure
(Research → Write-Tests → Implement) that IS the plan. Do not wrap it in a
separate planning workflow — execute the phases directly.

# External Agent Binary — Full Pipeline

Build a standalone external agent binary that implements the Entire CLI external agent protocol.

The current test split is:

1. **Protocol compliance** lives in `external-agents-tests`.
2. **Lifecycle integration** lives in this repo's `e2e/` harness.
3. **Unit tests** live in each agent module.

Do not add new generic protocol tests under this repo's `e2e/` directory.

## Parameters

Collect these before starting if the user did not provide them:

| Parameter | Description | Default |
|-----------|-------------|---------|
| `AGENT_NAME` | Human-readable name (for example, `Windsurf`) | User-provided |
| `AGENT_SLUG` | Binary suffix for `entire-agent-<slug>` | Kebab-case of `AGENT_NAME` |
| `LANGUAGE` | Implementation language | `Go` |
| `PROJECT_DIR` | Agent directory to create or edit | `./agents/entire-agent-<slug>` |
| `ENTIRE_BIN` | Path to the Entire CLI binary for lifecycle testing | `entire` from `PATH` or `E2E_ENTIRE_BIN` |

## Phase Selection

- `/entire-external-agent research` runs only Phase 1.
- `/entire-external-agent write-tests` runs only Phase 2.
- `/entire-external-agent implement` runs only Phase 3.
- `/entire-external-agent` runs all three phases in order.

If a single phase is requested, still collect the shared parameters first.

## Protocol Spec

Use the protocol specification at:
`https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/docs/architecture/external-agent-protocol.md`

If the user gives a different spec location explicitly, use that instead.

## Core Rule: Black-Box-First TDD

1. **Protocol compliance is the contract.** The binary must pass the shared `external-agents-tests` suite.
2. **Lifecycle tests prove real integration.** The repo-local `e2e/` harness covers the Entire + real-agent workflow and stays separate from generic protocol checks.
3. **Unit tests are written last.** After protocol and lifecycle behavior are working, add unit tests to lock down parsing, hooks, and file handling.
4. **Watch failures before fixing them.** Run the failing test first so you know what behavior the code must satisfy.
5. **Keep the fix scoped.** Implement only the behavior needed for the current failure, then rerun.

## Pipeline

### Phase 1: Research

Discover the target agent's hook mechanism, transcript format, session layout, CLI entrypoints, and lifecycle prerequisites. Produce `<PROJECT_DIR>/AGENT.md` with the protocol mapping and any real-CLI requirements needed for lifecycle tests.

Use `.claude/skills/entire-external-agent/research.md`.

Expected output:
- `<PROJECT_DIR>/AGENT.md`

### Phase 2: Write Tests

Scaffold the binary and the test surfaces you will need:

- agent module structure under `<PROJECT_DIR>`
- protocol compliance expectations compatible with `external-agents-tests`
- lifecycle adapter wiring in this repo's `e2e/` harness
- optional compliance fixtures if the agent benefits from stronger black-box detect or transcript assertions

Use `.claude/skills/entire-external-agent/write-tests.md`.

Expected output:
- compiling binary scaffold
- any needed lifecycle adapter files under `e2e/agents/`
- optional fixture file paths documented in `<PROJECT_DIR>/AGENT.md` or `README.md`

### Phase 3: Implement

Implement until:

- the binary passes protocol compliance
- lifecycle tests pass when the required CLIs are available
- unit tests cover the important internal behaviors

Use `.claude/skills/entire-external-agent/implement.md`.

Expected output:
- fully working binary
- passing unit tests
- passing protocol compliance
- passing lifecycle integration where dependencies are available

## Final Summary

At the end, summarize:

- agent name and binary name
- implementation language
- declared capabilities
- protocol compliance status
- lifecycle test status
- unit test coverage
- installation instructions
- any remaining gaps

# Contributing to Entire External Agents

Thank you for your interest in contributing! If the
[Entire CLI](https://github.com/entireio/cli) doesn't already integrate
with the AI coding agent you use, this is the repository where you can add
that support. Each integration lives in its own directory under
[`agents/`](agents/) as a small standalone Go program that lets Entire hook
into a specific coding tool to capture checkpoints, transcripts, and
lifecycle events.

**You don't have to build it from scratch.** This repo ships a built-in
skill that walks an AI coding tool (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, or
OpenCode) through researching the target agent, scaffolding tests, and
implementing the binary. It gets you most of the way to a working
integration. See [Adding a New External Agent](#adding-a-new-external-agent)
below for how to invoke it.

Please read the [Code of Conduct](https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md)
before participating.

> **New to Entire?** See the [First-Time Contributors guide](doc/first-time-contributors.md)
> for a step-by-step first contribution walkthrough, or the [README](README.md)
> for setup and architecture documentation.

---

## Before You Code: Discuss First

The fastest way to get a contribution merged is to align with maintainers before
writing code. Please **open an issue first** describing what you want to change,
and wait for maintainer feedback before starting implementation. This is
especially true if you want to add support for a brand-new agent. That work
is a long-running commitment the maintainers will need to support.

### Contribution Workflow

1. **Open an issue** describing the problem, feature, or new agent
2. **Wait for maintainer feedback**: we may have relevant context or plans
3. **Get approval** before starting implementation
4. **Submit your PR** referencing the approved issue
5. **Address all feedback** including automated Copilot comments
6. **Maintainer review and merge**

---

## First-Time Contributors

New to the project? Welcome! Start with the
[First-Time Contributors guide](doc/first-time-contributors.md). It explains
how to find a comfortable issue, ask for a scoped starter task, set up the
repo, use AI responsibly, run the right checks, and open your first pull
request.

### Starter Contributions

We recommend starting with:

- **A protocol compliance gap**: An existing agent fails a check against
  [`entireio/external-agents-tests`](https://github.com/entireio/external-agents-tests)
  on your machine; add a focused fix.
```

# Research Procedure

Assess a target agent's capabilities and produce a protocol mapping for building an external agent binary.

## Prerequisites

Ensure the following parameters are available (from the orchestrator or user):
- `AGENT_NAME` — Human-readable agent name
- `AGENT_SLUG` — Kebab-case slug for the binary name
- `PROJECT_DIR` — Where the project will be created

## Phase 1: Understand the External Agent Protocol

Read the protocol specification to understand what subcommands and response formats the binary must implement.

**Protocol spec:**
1. Read `https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/docs/architecture/external-agent-protocol.md` — full protocol spec (subcommands, JSON schemas, capabilities)
2. Read `https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/cmd/entire/cli/agent/external/types.go` — JSON response type definitions
3. Read `https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/cmd/entire/cli/agent/external/external.go` — how the CLI invokes each subcommand (args, stdin, expected stdout)

If the user provides a different protocol spec location explicitly, read that instead.

Key things to note:
- Which subcommands are always required vs. capability-gated
- The JSON schema for each response type
- The HookInput, AgentSession, and Event object formats
- Environment variables set on every invocation (`ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT`, `ENTIRE_PROTOCOL_VERSION`)

## Phase 2: Investigate the Target Agent

Ask the user: "Do you have documentation or specs for the target agent's hook/lifecycle system? Or should I auto-research?"

### If user provides docs:
Read the provided docs and extract:
- Hook/lifecycle mechanism (how to register callbacks)
- Session management (where sessions are stored, how IDs work)
- Transcript format and location
- Configuration file format and location

### If auto-research:

#### Step 1: Binary probing

Run non-destructive CLI checks. Record PASS/WARN/FAIL for each:

| Check | Command | PASS | FAIL |
|-------|---------|------|------|
| Binary present | `command -v <agent-binary>` | Found | Not found |
| Help output | `<agent-binary> --help` or `<agent-binary> help` | Available | No help |
| Version info | `<agent-binary> --version` | Available | N/A |
| Hook keywords | Scan help for: hook, lifecycle, callback, event, trigger, pre-, post-, plugin, extension | Found | None found |
| Session keywords | Scan help for: session, resume, continue, history, transcript, context | Found | None found |
| Config directory | Check `~/.<agent-slug>/`, `~/.config/<agent-slug>/`, `./<agent-slug>/`, `./.${agent-slug}/` | Found | None found |

#### Step 2: Documentation search

Use web search to find:
- The agent's official hook/plugin/extension documentation
- How to register lifecycle callbacks
- Session/transcript storage format and location
- Configuration file format

#### Step 3: Config and session directory exploration

If a config directory was found:
1. List its contents (non-destructively)
2. Look for settings files (JSON, YAML, TOML)
3. Look for session/history directories
4. Look for transcript files and determine their format

#### Step 4: Map agent concepts to protocol subcommands

For each protocol subcommand, determine:
- Whether the agent has a native concept that maps to it
- How to implement it (which native API/config/file to use)
- Whether it's straightforward or requires workarounds

Create a mapping table:

| Subcommand | Native Concept | Implementation Notes | Feasibility |
|-----------|---------------|---------------------|-------------|
| `info` | — | Static metadata, always implementable | Required |
| `detect` | — | Check for binary or config | Required |
| `get-session-id` | (agent's session ID mechanism) | ... | Required |
| `get-session-dir` | (agent's session directory) | ... | Required |
| `resolve-session-file` | (how agent stores sessions) | ... | Required |
| `read-session` | (session data format) | ... | Required |
| `write-session` | (session data persistence) | ... | Required |
| `read-transcript` | (transcript file location) | ... | Required |
| `chunk-transcript` | (raw bytes, language-generic) | Base64 chunking | Required |
| `reassemble-transcript` | (reverse of chunk) | Base64 reassembly | Required |
| `format-resume-command` | (agent's resume mechanism) | ... | Required |
| `parse-hook` | (agent's native hook events) | ... | If hooks capable |
| `install-hooks` | (agent's hook config format) | ... | If hooks capable |
| `uninstall-hooks` | (reverse of install) | ... | If hooks capable |
| `are-hooks-installed` | (check hook config) | ... | If hooks capable |
| `get-transcript-position` | (file size/position) | ... | If transcript analyzer |
| `extract-modified-files` | (parse transcript for file ops) | ... | If transcript analyzer |
| `extract-prompts` | (parse transcript for user msgs) | ... | If transcript analyzer |
| `extract-summary` | (parse transcript for summaries) | ... | If transcript analyzer |

#### Step 5: Select capabilities

Based on the mapping, determine which capabilities the binary should declare:
- `hooks` — Can the agent be configured to call external commands on lifecycle events?
- `transcript_analyzer` — Does the agent produce parseable transcripts?
- `transcript_preparer` — Does the transcript need pre-processing?
- `token_calculator` — Does the transcript contain token usage data?
- `text_generator` — Can the agent's LLM be invoked for text generation?
- `hook_response_writer` — Does the agent support writing messages back via hooks?
- `subagent_aware_extractor` — Does the agent spawn subagents with their own transcripts?

## Phase 3: Verification Script

Docs tend to be out of date. Before writing the one-pager, create a verification script that captures real payloads from the target agent to confirm Phase 2 findings.

Based on Phase 2 findings, create an **agent-specific** test script:

```
<PROJECT_DIR>/scripts/verify-<AGENT_SLUG>.sh
```

The script is tailored to the specific agent's hook mechanism (not a generic template). Adapt the hook wiring section based on what Phase 2 discovered.

**Script structure:**

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

AGENT_NAME="..."
AGENT_SLUG="..."
AGENT_BIN="..."
PROBE_DIR="<PROJECT_DIR>/.probe-${AGENT_SLUG}-$(date +%s)"
```

**Required sections:**

1. **Static checks** — Re-runnable binary/version/help checks from Phase 2
2. **Hook wiring** — Create workspace-local config that intercepts the agent's hooks and dumps stdin JSON to `$PROBE_DIR/captures/<event-name>-<timestamp>.json`. Use the agent's native hook config format discovered in Phase 2.
3. **Run modes:**
   - `--run-cmd '<cmd>'` — Automated: launch agent with a non-interactive prompt, wait, collect captures
```

> AGENT

Short answer: **no, you don't need the external agent's source repo** — but you do need its **CLI installed and working on your machine**, because the process is black-box: you probe the real binary, inspect its config/session files, and run lifecycle tests against the actual tool.

Here's what you need, based on this repo's README, CONTRIBUTING.md, and the `/entire-external-agent` skill:

**What you need to have**

1. **The target agent's CLI installed locally** — the research phase runs `--help`/`--version` probes, scans for hook/session keywords, and explores its config dirs (`~/.config/<agent>/` etc.). The `e2e/` lifecycle tests also drive the real CLI (prompt execution, checkpoints, rewind), so you may need an account/API key for that agent too.
2. **The Entire CLI binary** (`entire` on PATH, or set via `E2E_ENTIRE_BIN`) for lifecycle testing.
3. **Go toolchain + mise** — each agent is a standalone Go program under `agents/entire-agent-<slug>/` with its own `mise.toml` defining `build` and `test` tasks (that's the contract CI uses to discover it).
4. **Docs for the agent's hook/lifecycle system** if they exist — the skill will ask whether you have docs or want it to auto-research via web search. Either works; having docs just speeds things up.

**What you need to know (or will discover during research)**

- How the agent registers **hooks/lifecycle callbacks** (config file format)
- Where it stores **sessions** and how session IDs work
- Its **transcript format and location**, and whether transcripts contain token usage data
- How its **resume** mechanism works — these all map to the ~20 protocol subcommands (`detect`, `read-transcript`, `install-hooks`, etc.) and determine which capabilities your binary declares

**Process notes**

- Per `CONTRIBUTING.md`, **open an issue first and get maintainer approval** before building a new agent — it's treated as a long-running support commitment.
- The protocol spec lives in the `entireio/cli` repo (`docs/architecture/external-agent-protocol.md`) — that's the one external repo you'll read, along with `entireio/external-agents-tests` for the shared compliance suite (both public).
- Then `/entire-external-agent` runs the three phases (research → write-tests → implement) and produces `AGENT.md`, the scaffold, and the implementation. Kiro and Amp under `agents/` are good reference examples.

The one practical blocker to check up front: confirm the agent actually **supports external hooks/callbacks and produces readable transcripts**. If it has no hook mechanism and no on-disk session/transcript files, the integration may not be feasible — that's exactly what the research phase is designed to determine before you commit to building.

```
150	   - `--run-cmd '<cmd>'` — Automated: launch agent with a non-interactive prompt, wait, collect captures
151	   - `--manual-live` — Interactive: user runs agent manually, presses Enter when done
152	4. **Capture collection** — List and pretty-print all captured payload files
153	5. **Cleanup** — Restore original config (unless `--keep-config`)
154	6. **Verdict** — PASS/WARN/FAIL per lifecycle event + summary
155	
156	**Important:** The hook wiring must be non-destructive — back up any existing config before modifying, and restore it on cleanup.
157	
158	### Phase 3a: Execute & Analyze
159	
160	Run the script and analyze the captured payloads:
161	
162	1. **Execute**: `chmod +x <PROJECT_DIR>/scripts/verify-<AGENT_SLUG>.sh && <PROJECT_DIR>/scripts/verify-<AGENT_SLUG>.sh --manual-live`
163	2. **For each captured payload**: show the file path, decoded JSON, and which protocol subcommand it informs
164	3. **Update Phase 2 findings**: If captured payloads differ from documentation (field names, nesting, missing fields), update the mapping table with ground-truth values from the captures
165	4. **Lifecycle mapping**: native hook name → protocol event type, validated against real captures
166	
167	If the script cannot be run (agent not installed, auth required, sandbox restrictions), follow the Blocker Handling procedure and note in the one-pager that findings are doc-based only and not verified.
168	
169	### Phase 3b: Verify Stored Data Completeness
170	
171	Hook payloads tell you *when* events fire, but not whether the agent's stored data (sessions, transcripts) actually contains usable content. IDEs often store placeholder text in session files while keeping the real data in a separate location (execution logs, databases, caches). **You must verify this before writing the one-pager.**
172	
173	#### Step 1: Use distinctive prompts in a test repo
174	
175	Create a test repo and run 2-3 prompts with greppable content:
176	- Ask the agent to summarize a URL (produces a known response)
177	- Ask it to create a file (produces tool calls + file writes)
178	- Ask a follow-up question (produces a multi-turn session)
179	
180	These give you specific strings to search for across all storage locations.
181	
182	#### Step 2: Inspect session/transcript files for actual content
183	
184	Read the session files identified in Phase 2 and check each assistant message:
185	- Does it contain the **actual response text** you saw in the UI, or just a placeholder like `"On it."`, `"Working..."`, `"Sure"`, or empty string?
186	- Are **tool calls** recorded with their names, inputs, and outputs?
187	- Is the `completion` / `response` / `content` field populated or empty?
188	- Do `promptLogs` or similar fields contain the full model output?
189	
190	**If assistant content is placeholder-only, the agent stores real data elsewhere.** Proceed to Step 3.
191	
192	#### Step 3: Find ALL recently modified files
193	
194	Before running a prompt, create a timestamp marker. After the prompt completes, find everything the IDE touched:
195	
196	```bash
197	touch /tmp/before-marker
198	# ... run your prompt in the IDE ...
199	find ~/Library/Application\ Support/<agent>/ -type f -newer /tmp/before-marker 2>/dev/null
200	# Linux: find ~/.config/<agent>/ -type f -newer /tmp/before-marker
201	```
202	
203	This reveals hidden storage that documentation may not mention — execution logs, per-turn action traces, content-addressed caches, etc.
204	
205	#### Step 4: Search for your distinctive response
206	
207	Grep the entire IDE data directory for the known response text from your test prompts:
208	
209	```bash
210	grep -rl "your distinctive response text" ~/Library/Application\ Support/<agent>/ 2>/dev/null
211	```
212	
213	This definitively locates where the real data lives. Read the matching files to understand their format.
214	
215	#### Step 5: Map the IDE's internal ID system
216	
217	Look for cross-referencing IDs that link session files to the real data:
218	- Session IDs, execution IDs, conversation IDs, workspace IDs
219	- How they cross-reference (e.g., session file has `executionId` → execution log has matching field)
220	- Whether IDs are deterministic (computable from workspace path or session ID) or opaque (require scanning)
221	- Whether directory names are hashes, base64-encoded paths, or opaque
222	
223	#### Step 6: Verify hook data flow
224	
225	Confirm hooks actually receive the data you expect:
226	- Create a diagnostic hook that dumps everything to a file: `sh -c 'cat > /tmp/hook-dump-$(date +%s).json'`
227	- For each hook type, check: Does stdin contain JSON? Are env vars set? Are args passed?
228	- **Watch for stdin redirects** — some IDEs wrap hook commands in `sh -c '... </dev/null'` which blocks stdin data flow
229	- If hooks receive empty input, determine if the IDE passes data via environment variables, command arguments, or not at all
230	
231	#### Step 7: Document findings
232	
233	Record what you found for the one-pager:
234	- Primary storage: where session/conversation state lives (even if incomplete)
235	- Secondary storage: where the real response/tool data lives (execution logs, databases, etc.)
236	- Cross-reference mechanism: how to link primary → secondary (IDs, timestamps, etc.)
237	- Hook data availability: what each hook type actually receives
238	
239	## Phase 4: Write the One-Pager
240	
241	Create `<PROJECT_DIR>/AGENT.md` (create the directory first if needed).
242	
243	**Important:** Use ground-truth values from verification script captures (Phase 3a) wherever available. If a field was verified by real payloads, mark it as `(verified)`. If based only on docs/probing, mark it as `(unverified)`.
244	
245	Template:
246	
247	```markdown
248	# <AGENT_NAME> — External Agent Research
249	
250	## Verdict: COMPATIBLE / PARTIAL / INCOMPATIBLE
251	
252	## Static Checks
253	| Check | Result | Notes |
254	|-------|--------|-------|
255	| Binary present | PASS/FAIL | path |
256	| Help available | PASS/FAIL | |
257	| Version info | PASS/FAIL | version string |
258	| Hook keywords | PASS/FAIL | keywords found |
259	| Session keywords | PASS/FAIL | keywords found |
260	| Config directory | PASS/FAIL | path |
261	| Documentation | PASS/FAIL | URL |
262	
263	## Binary
264	- Name: `<agent-binary>`
265	- Version: ...
266	- Install: ... (how to install if not present)
267	
268	## Hook Mechanism
269	- Config file: (exact path)
270	- Config format: JSON / YAML / TOML
271	- Hook registration: (how hooks are declared)
272	- Hook names and protocol mapping:
273	  | Native Hook Name | When It Fires | Protocol Event Type |
274	  |-----------------|---------------|---------------------|
275	  | ... | ... | 1=SessionStart, 2=TurnStart, etc. |
276	- Hook input format: (what data is passed to hooks — stdin, env vars, args)
277	
278	## Session Management
279	- Session directory: (where sessions are stored)
280	- Session ID source: (how to extract from hook input or filesystem)
281	- Session file format: (JSON, JSONL, binary, etc.)
282	
283	## Transcript
284	- Location: (path pattern)
285	- Format: JSONL / JSON array / other
286	- User prompt field: (which JSON field contains user prompts)
287	- Modified files field: (which JSON field contains file operations)
288	- Token usage field: (if available)
289	- Example entry: `{"role": "user", "content": "..."}`
290	
291	## Data Storage Verification
292	- Session files contain actual assistant content: YES / NO — PLACEHOLDER ONLY
293	- If placeholder: what do session files contain instead? (e.g., "On it.", empty completion field)
294	- Secondary storage location: (path pattern for execution logs, databases, etc. — or "none found")
295	- Secondary storage format: (JSON per execution, SQLite, JSONL, etc.)
296	- Cross-reference key: (how session entries link to secondary storage, e.g., executionId field)
297	- Hook data flow verified: YES / BROKEN (does hook stdin/env actually contain expected data?)
298	- If broken: what blocks data flow? (e.g., `</dev/null` redirect, missing env vars)
299	- Verification method: (grep for known response, find recently modified files, diagnostic hook dump)
300	
301	## Protocol Mapping
302	| Subcommand | Native Concept | Implementation Notes | Feasibility |
303	|-----------|---------------|---------------------|-------------|
304	| ... | ... | ... | ... |
305	
306	## Selected Capabilities
307	| Capability | Declared | Justification |
308	|-----------|----------|---------------|
309	| hooks | true/false | ... |
310	| transcript_analyzer | true/false | ... |
311	| transcript_preparer | true/false | ... |
312	| token_calculator | true/false | ... |
313	| text_generator | true/false | ... |
314	| hook_response_writer | true/false | ... |
315	| subagent_aware_extractor | true/false | ... |
316	
317	## Gaps & Limitations
318	- ... (anything that doesn't map cleanly or requires workarounds)
319	- Note if session files contain placeholder content instead of real responses
320	- Note if hooks receive empty stdin or missing env vars
321	- Note if secondary storage requires directory scanning (opaque hashes, etc.)
322	
323	## Captured Payloads
324	- Verification script: `<PROJECT_DIR>/scripts/verify-<AGENT_SLUG>.sh`
325	- Capture directory: `<PROJECT_DIR>/.probe-<AGENT_SLUG>-*/captures/`
326	- Verification status: VERIFIED (script ran, payloads captured) / UNVERIFIED (doc-based only)
327	- Notable differences from docs: ... (any discrepancies found between docs and real payloads)
328	
329	## E2E Test Prerequisites
330	- Entire CLI binary: (how to obtain/path — default: `entire` from PATH or `E2E_ENTIRE_BIN` env)
331	- Agent CLI binary: (name, path, how to install)
332	- Non-interactive prompt command: (exact command + flags to send a prompt without interactive UI)
333	- Interactive mode: (supported? exact command to launch interactive session)
334	- Expected prompt pattern: (regex for tmux WaitFor — what the agent's prompt looks like)
335	- Timeout multiplier: (1.0 for fast agents, higher for slow APIs)
336	- Bootstrap steps: (any auth/setup needed before tests run)
337	- Transient error patterns: (strings that indicate retryable API errors, e.g., "overloaded", "rate limit", "503", "529")
338	```
339	
340	Fill in every section with concrete values from the investigation. Don't leave placeholders. If a section doesn't apply, say so explicitly.
341	
342	## Phase 5: Commit
343	
344	Create a git commit for the `AGENT.md` file and the verification script.
345	
346	## Blocker Handling
347	
348	If blocked at any point (auth, sandbox, binary not found):
349	
350	1. State the exact blocker
351	2. Provide the exact command for the user to run manually
352	3. Explain what output to paste back
353	4. Continue with provided output
354	
355	## Constraints
356	
357	- **No implementation code.** This phase produces only `AGENT.md` and the verification script.
358	- **Non-destructive.** All probing is read-only — don't modify agent config. The verification script backs up and restores any config it touches.
359	- **Verify before documenting.** Prefer ground-truth from captured payloads over documentation claims. Mark unverified findings explicitly.
360	- **Ask, don't assume.** If the hook mechanism is unclear, ask the user.
361

```
1	# External Agent Plugin Protocol
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	The Entire CLI supports external agent plugins — standalone binaries that implement the Agent interface via a subcommand-based protocol over stdin/stdout. This allows third-party agents to integrate with the CLI without modifying the main repository.
6	
7	## Discovery
8	
9	The CLI discovers external agents by scanning `$PATH` for executables matching the pattern `entire-agent-<name>`. For example, `entire-agent-cursor` would register as the "cursor" agent.
10	
11	- Binaries whose `<name>` conflicts with an already-registered built-in agent are skipped.
12	- Discovery runs once during CLI initialization (before building the hooks command tree).
13	- The binary must be executable and respond to the `info` subcommand.
14	
15	## Environment
16	
17	Every subcommand invocation sets:
18	
19	| Variable | Description |
20	|---|---|
21	| `ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT` | Absolute path to the git repository root |
22	| `ENTIRE_PROTOCOL_VERSION` | Protocol version (`1`) |
23	
24	The working directory is set to the repository root.
25	
26	## Communication Model
27	
28	- **Subcommand-based:** Each Agent interface method maps to a CLI subcommand.
29	- **JSON over stdin/stdout:** Structured data uses JSON. Transcripts use raw bytes.
30	- **Stateless:** Each invocation is independent — no persistent connection.
31	- **Exit codes:** `0` = success, non-zero = error. Error messages go to stderr.
32	
33	## Subcommands
34	
35	### Always Required
36	
37	#### `info`
38	
39	Returns agent metadata and declared capabilities.
40	
41	**Arguments:** None
42	
43	**Output (stdout):** JSON
44	
45	```json
46	{
47	  "protocol_version": 1,
48	  "name": "cursor",
49	  "type": "Cursor",
50	  "description": "Cursor - AI-powered code editor",
51	  "is_preview": true,
52	  "protected_dirs": [".cursor"],
53	  "hook_names": ["session-start", "session-end", "stop"],
54	  "capabilities": {
55	    "hooks": true,
56	    "transcript_analyzer": true,
57	    "transcript_preparer": false,
58	    "token_calculator": false,
59	    "text_generator": false,
60	    "hook_response_writer": false,
61	    "subagent_aware_extractor": false
62	  }
63	}
64	```
65	
66	The `capabilities` object determines which optional subcommands the CLI will call. If a capability is `false` or missing, the CLI will never invoke the corresponding subcommands.
67	
68	#### `detect`
69	
70	Checks whether the agent is present/usable in the current environment.
71	
72	**Arguments:** None
73	
74	**Output (stdout):** JSON
75	
76	```json
77	{"present": true}
78	```
79	
80	#### `get-session-id`
81	
82	Extracts the session ID from a hook input event.
83	
84	**Input (stdin):** JSON — the [HookInput object](#hookinput-object)
85	
86	**Output (stdout):** JSON
87	
88	```json
89	{"session_id": "abc123"}
90	```
91	
92	#### `get-session-dir --repo-path <path>`
93	
94	Returns the directory where agent sessions are stored.
95	
96	**Arguments:**
97	- `--repo-path` — Absolute path to the repository
98	
99	**Output (stdout):** JSON
100	
101	```json
102	{"session_dir": "/path/to/sessions"}
103	```
104	
105	#### `resolve-session-file --session-dir <dir> --session-id <id>`
106	
107	Resolves the session file path from a session directory and ID.
108	
109	**Arguments:**
110	- `--session-dir` — Session directory path
111	- `--session-id` — Session identifier
112	
113	**Output (stdout):** JSON
114	
115	```json
116	{"session_file": "/path/to/session/file.jsonl"}
117	```
118	
119	#### `read-session`
120	
121	Reads session data from a hook input event.
122	
123	**Input (stdin):** JSON — the [HookInput object](#hookinput-object)
124	
125	**Output (stdout):** JSON — [AgentSession object](#agentsession-object)
126	
127	```json
128	{
129	  "session_id": "abc123",
130	  "agent_name": "cursor",
131	  "repo_path": "/path/to/repo",
132	  "session_ref": "/path/to/file.jsonl",
133	  "start_time": "2026-01-13T12:00:00Z",
134	  "native_data": null,
135	  "modified_files": ["src/main.go"],
136	  "new_files": [],
137	  "deleted_files": []
138	}
139	```
140	
141	#### `write-session`
142	
143	Writes/updates session data.
144	
145	**Input (stdin):** JSON — the [AgentSession object](#agentsession-object)
146	
147	**Output:** Exit 0 on success.
148	
149	#### `read-transcript --session-ref <path>`
150	
151	Reads a transcript file and returns its raw bytes.
152	
153	**Arguments:**
154	- `--session-ref` — Path to the transcript file
155	
156	**Output (stdout):** Raw transcript bytes.
157	
158	#### `chunk-transcript --max-size <n>`
159	
160	Splits a transcript into chunks for storage.
161	
162	**Input (stdin):** Raw transcript bytes.
163	
164	**Arguments:**
165	- `--max-size` — Maximum chunk size in bytes
166	
167	**Output (stdout):** JSON
168	
169	```json
170	{"chunks": ["<base64-encoded-chunk>", "..."]}
171	```
172	
173	#### `reassemble-transcript`
174	
175	Reassembles transcript chunks back into a full transcript.
176	
177	**Input (stdin):** JSON
178	
179	```json
180	{"chunks": ["<base64-encoded-chunk>", "..."]}
181	```
182	
183	**Output (stdout):** Raw transcript bytes.
184	
185	#### `format-resume-command --session-id <id>`
186	
187	Returns the command a user can run to resume a session.
188	
189	**Arguments:**
190	- `--session-id` — Session identifier
191	
192	**Output (stdout):** JSON
193	
194	```json
195	{"command": "cursor --resume abc123"}
196	```
197	
198	### Capability: `hooks`
199	
200	These subcommands are required when `capabilities.hooks` is `true`.
201	
202	#### `parse-hook --hook <name>`
203	
204	Parses a raw agent hook payload into a structured event.
205	
206	**Arguments:**
207	- `--hook` — Hook name (e.g., "stop", "session-start")
208	
209	**Input (stdin):** Raw agent hook payload bytes.
210	
211	**Output (stdout):** JSON — the parsed [Event object](#event-object), or `null` if the payload is not relevant.
212	
213	#### `install-hooks [--local-dev] [--force]`
214	
215	Installs agent hooks for Entire integration.
216	
217	**Arguments:**
218	- `--local-dev` — Use local development binary path (optional)
219	- `--force` — Overwrite existing hooks (optional)
220	
221	**Output (stdout):** JSON
222	
223	```json
224	{"hooks_installed": 3}
225	```
226	
227	#### `uninstall-hooks`
228	
229	Removes installed agent hooks.
230	
231	**Arguments:** None
232	
233	**Output:** Exit 0 on success.
234	
235	#### `are-hooks-installed`
236	
237	Checks whether hooks are currently installed.
238	
239	**Arguments:** None
240	
241	**Output (stdout):** JSON
242	
243	```json
244	{"installed": true}
245	```
246	
247	### Capability: `transcript_analyzer`
248	
249	Required when `capabilities.transcript_analyzer` is `true`.
250	
251	#### `get-transcript-position --path <path>`
252	
253	Returns the current byte position/size of a transcript file.
254	
255	**Arguments:**
256	- `--path` — Path to the transcript file
257	
258	**Output (stdout):** JSON
259	
260	```json
261	{"position": 12345}
262	```
263	
264	#### `extract-modified-files --path <path> --offset <n>`
265	
266	Extracts the list of files modified by the agent from a transcript.
267	
268	**Arguments:**
269	- `--path` — Path to the transcript file
270	- `--offset` — Byte offset to start reading from
271	
272	**Output (stdout):** JSON
273	
274	```json
275	{"files": ["path/to/file1.go", "path/to/file2.go"], "current_position": 12345}
276	```
277	
278	The `current_position` field returns the transcript position after extraction, allowing the caller to resume from that point on subsequent calls.
279	
280	#### `extract-prompts --session-ref <path> --offset <n>`
281	
282	Extracts user prompts from a transcript.
283	
284	**Arguments:**
285	- `--session-ref` — Path to the transcript file
286	- `--offset` — Byte offset to start reading from
287	
288	**Output (stdout):** JSON
289	
290	```json
291	{"prompts": ["first prompt text", "second prompt text"]}
292	```
293	
294	#### `extract-summary --session-ref <path>`
295	
296	Extracts an AI-generated summary from a transcript.
297	
298	**Arguments:**
299	- `--session-ref` — Path to the transcript file
300	
301	**Output (stdout):** JSON
302	
303	```json
304	{"summary": "Summary text here", "has_summary": true}
305	```
306	
307	### Capability: `transcript_preparer`
308	
309	Required when `capabilities.transcript_preparer` is `true`.
310	
311	#### `prepare-transcript --session-ref <path>`
312	
313	Prepares/processes a transcript file (e.g., converting from raw format).
314	
315	**Arguments:**
316	- `--session-ref` — Path to the transcript file
317	
318	**Output:** Exit 0 on success.
319	
320	### Capability: `token_calculator`
321	
322	Required when `capabilities.token_calculator` is `true`.
323	
324	#### `calculate-tokens --offset <n>`
325	
326	Calculates token usage from a transcript.
327	
328	**Input (stdin):** Raw transcript bytes.
329	
330	**Arguments:**
331	- `--offset` — Byte offset to start calculating from
332	
333	**Output (stdout):** JSON
334	
335	```json
336	{
337	  "input_tokens": 1500,
338	  "cache_creation_tokens": 0,
339	  "cache_read_tokens": 200,
340	  "output_tokens": 500,
341	  "api_call_count": 3,
342	  "subagent_tokens": null
343	}
344	```
345	
346	Only `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` are required. The optional fields (`cache_creation_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `api_call_count`) default to `0` if omitted. `subagent_tokens` is an optional nested object with the same structure, for agents that spawn subagents.
347	
348	### Capability: `text_generator`
349	
350	Required when `capabilities.text_generator` is `true`.
351	
352	External agents that declare `capabilities.text_generator: true` can be used as
353	summary providers for `entire explain --generate` and checkpoint auto-summarize.
354	Configure them with:
355	
356	```bash
357	entire configure --summarize-provider <agent-name> [--summarize-model <model>]
358	```
359	
360	The provider name is derived from the executable name `entire-agent-<agent-name>`.
361	The CLI passes Entire's summary prompt on stdin to `generate-text --model <model>`
362	and expects `{"text":"..."}` on stdout.
363	
364	#### `generate-text --model <model>`
365	
366	Generates text using the agent's underlying LLM.
367	
368	**Input (stdin):** Prompt text.
369	
370	**Arguments:**
371	- `--model` — Model to use for generation
372	
373	**Output (stdout):** JSON
374	
375	```json
376	{"text": "Generated response text"}
377	```
378	
379	### Capability: `hook_response_writer`
380	
381	Required when `capabilities.hook_response_writer` is `true`.
382	
383	#### `write-hook-response --message <message>`
384	
385	Writes a message in the agent's native hook response format.
386	
387	**Arguments:**
388	- `--message` — Message to write
389	
390	**Output (stdout):** Agent-native format bytes (e.g., JSONL for Claude Code).
391	
392	### Capability: `subagent_aware_extractor`
393	
394	Required when `capabilities.subagent_aware_extractor` is `true`.
395	
396	#### `extract-all-modified-files --offset <n> --subagents-dir <dir>`
397	
398	Extracts modified files from both the main transcript and any subagent transcripts.
399	
400	**Input (stdin):** Raw main transcript bytes.
401	
402	**Arguments:**
403	- `--offset` — Byte offset for the main transcript
404	- `--subagents-dir` — Directory containing subagent transcripts
405	
406	**Output (stdout):** JSON
407	
408	```json
409	{"files": ["file1.go", "file2.go"]}
410	```
411	
412	#### `calculate-total-tokens --offset <n> --subagents-dir <dir>`
413	
414	Calculates total token usage across main transcript and subagent transcripts.
415	
416	**Input (stdin):** Raw main transcript bytes.
417	
418	**Arguments:**
419	- `--offset` — Byte offset for the main transcript
420	- `--subagents-dir` — Directory containing subagent transcripts
421	
422	**Output (stdout):** JSON
423	
424	```json
425	{
426	  "input_tokens": 5000,
427	  "cache_creation_tokens": 0,
428	  "cache_read_tokens": 1000,
429	  "output_tokens": 2000,
430	  "api_call_count": 8,
431	  "subagent_tokens": {
432	    "input_tokens": 2000,
433	    "output_tokens": 800
434	  }
435	}
436	```
437	
438	Same token usage format as `calculate-tokens`. The `subagent_tokens` field aggregates usage from all subagents.
439	
440	## Shared Object Definitions
441	
442	### HookInput Object
443	
444	The HookInput object is passed via stdin to `get-session-id` and `read-session`.
445	
446	```json
447	{
448	  "hook_type": "stop",
449	  "session_id": "abc123",
450	  "session_ref": "/path/to/transcript.jsonl",
451	  "timestamp": "2026-01-13T12:00:00Z",
452	  "user_prompt": "Fix the login bug",
453	  "tool_name": "Write",
454	  "tool_use_id": "toolu_abc123",
455	  "tool_input": {"path": "/src/main.go"},
456	  "raw_data": {"custom_field": "value"}
457	}
458	```
459	
460	| Field | Type | Description |
461	|---|---|---|
462	| `hook_type` | string | Hook type: `session_start`, `session_end`, `user_prompt_submit`, `stop`, `pre_tool_use`, `post_tool_use` |
463	| `session_id` | string | Agent session identifier |
464	| `session_ref` | string | Agent-specific session reference (typically a file path) |
465	| `timestamp` | string | RFC 3339 timestamp |
466	| `user_prompt` | string | User's prompt text (from `user_prompt_submit` hooks). Optional. |
467	| `tool_name` | string | Tool name (from `pre_tool_use`/`post_tool_use` hooks). Optional. |
468	| `tool_use_id` | string | Tool invocation ID. Optional. |
469	| `tool_input` | object | Raw tool input JSON. Optional. |
470	| `raw_data` | object | Agent-specific data for extension. Optional. |
471	
472	### AgentSession Object
473	
474	Used as input to `write-session` and output from `read-session`.
475	
476	```json
477	{
478	  "session_id": "abc123",
479	  "agent_name": "cursor",
480	  "repo_path": "/path/to/repo",
481	  "session_ref": "/path/to/transcript.jsonl",
482	  "start_time": "2026-01-13T12:00:00Z",
483	  "native_data": null,
484	  "modified_files": ["src/main.go"],
485	  "new_files": ["src/new_file.go"],
486	  "deleted_files": []
487	}
488	```
489	
490	| Field | Type | Description |
491	|---|---|---|
492	| `session_id` | string | Agent session identifier |
493	| `agent_name` | string | Agent registry name |
494	| `repo_path` | string | Absolute path to the repository |
495	| `session_ref` | string | Path/reference to session in agent's storage |
496	| `start_time` | string | RFC 3339 timestamp of session start |
497	| `native_data` | bytes/null | Session content in agent's native format (opaque to CLI) |
498	| `modified_files` | string[] | Files modified during the session |
499	| `new_files` | string[] | Files created during the session |
500	| `deleted_files` | string[] | Files deleted during the session |
501	
502	### Event Object
503	
504	Returned by `parse-hook`. Represents a normalized lifecycle event.
505	
506	```json
507	{
508	  "type": 3,
509	  "session_id": "abc123",
510	  "session_ref": "/path/to/transcript.jsonl",
511	  "prompt": "Fix the login bug",
512	  "model": "claude-sonnet-4-20250514",
513	  "timestamp": "2026-01-13T12:00:00Z"
514	}
515	```
516	
517	| Field | Type | Description |
518	|---|---|---|
519	| `type` | int | **Required.** Event type (see table below) |
520	| `session_id` | string | **Required.** Agent session identifier |
521	| `previous_session_id` | string | Non-empty when this event represents a session continuation/handoff. Optional. |
522	| `session_ref` | string | Agent-specific transcript reference. Optional. |
523	| `prompt` | string | User's prompt text (on `TurnStart` events). Optional. |
524	| `model` | string | LLM model identifier. Optional. |
525	| `timestamp` | string | RFC 3339 timestamp. Optional. |
526	| `tool_use_id` | string | Tool invocation ID (for `SubagentStart`/`SubagentEnd`). Optional. |
527	| `subagent_id` | string | Subagent instance ID (for `SubagentEnd`). Optional. |
528	| `tool_input` | object | Raw tool input JSON (for subagent type/description extraction). Optional. |
529	| `subagent_type` | string | Kind of subagent (for `SubagentStart`/`SubagentEnd`). Optional. |
530	| `task_description` | string | Subagent task description. Optional. |
531	| `response_message` | string | Message to display to the user via the agent. Optional. |
532	| `metadata` | object | Agent-specific state preserved across events. Optional. |
533	
534	**Event types:**
535	
536	| Value | Name | Description |
537	|---|---|---|
538	| 1 | SessionStart | Agent session has begun |
539	| 2 | TurnStart | User submitted a prompt, agent is about to work |
540	| 3 | TurnEnd | Agent finished responding to a prompt |
541	| 4 | Compaction | Agent is compressing its context window (triggers save + offset reset) |
542	| 5 | SessionEnd | Session has been terminated |
543	| 6 | SubagentStart | A subagent (task) has been spawned |
544	| 7 | SubagentEnd | A subagent (task) has completed |
545	
546	## Error Handling
547	
548	- Exit code `0` indicates success.
549	- Any non-zero exit code indicates an error.
550	- Error messages should be written to stderr.
551	- The CLI captures stderr and wraps it in a Go error.
552	- If the binary is not found in PATH, the agent is simply not registered.
553	- If `info` fails or returns invalid JSON, the binary is skipped during discovery.
554	
555	## Versioning
556	
557	The protocol version is declared in the `info` response (`protocol_version` field) and set via `ENTIRE_PROTOCOL_VERSION` environment variable. The CLI checks that the binary's protocol version matches its expected version before registering the agent.
558	
559	Future protocol versions may add new subcommands or capabilities. Existing subcommands will maintain backwards compatibility within a major version.
560	
```

## COMPREHENSIVE EXPLORATION REPORT: GOOSE AI CODING AGENT

Based on thorough exploration of the Goose codebase (Rust 1.37.0), here are your six questions answered with concrete file paths and code references:

---

### 1. HOOKS/LIFECYCLE MECHANISM

**YES, Goose has a comprehensive lifecycle hooks system** following the Open Plugins specification.

**File Locations:**
- **Main implementation:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs`
- **Agent integration:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/agents/agent.rs` (lines 244, 366, 487-552)
- **Plugin discovery:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/plugins/discovery.rs`

**Hook Configuration Format:**

Hooks are declared in **`<plugin-root>/hooks/hooks.json`** in each discovered plugin. The schema is:

```json
{
  "hooks": {
    "PostToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "developer__shell|developer__text_editor",
        "hooks": [
          { "type": "command", "command": "${PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/log.sh" }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

**Available Lifecycle Events** (from `HookEvent` enum in `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs` lines 49-63):
- `PreToolUse` - Before tool is executed
- `PostToolUse` - After successful tool use
- `PostToolUseFailure` - After tool use fails
- `SessionStart` - Session starts
- `SessionEnd` - Session ends
- `UserPromptSubmit` - User submits prompt
- `BeforeReadFile` - Before file read operation
- `AfterFileEdit` - After file edit operation
- `BeforeShellExecution` - Before shell command execution
- `AfterShellExecution` - After shell command execution
- `Stop` - Stop event
- `SubagentStart` - Subagent starts
- `SubagentStop` - Subagent stops

**Data Passed to Hooks:**

Hook scripts receive JSON context on stdin. The `HookContext` struct (`/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/hooks/mod.rs` lines 160-175) contains:

```rust
pub struct HookContext {
    pub event: String,              // Event name
    pub session_id: String,         // Session ID
    pub matcher_context: Option<String>,  // Tool name / file path / command
    pub tool_name: Option<String>,
    pub tool_input: Option<Value>,  // JSON tool arguments
    pub tool_output: Option<Value>, // JSON tool output
    pub message: Option<String>,
    pub working_dir: Option<String>,
}
```

**Hook Command Execution:**
- Hook scripts are executed via shell: `sh -c <command>`
- Environment variable `PLUGIN_ROOT` is set pointing to the plugin root
- Command text supports `${PLUGIN_ROOT}` placeholder substitution
- Timeout: default 30 seconds (configurable per hook via `timeout` field)
- Exit code 0 = success; any other code = failure (logged, not fatal)
- For blocking hooks: exit code 2 or JSON `{"decision":"block","reason":"..."}` to deny

**Blocking vs. Non-blocking:**
- `emit()` - Non-blocking (errors logged, never propagated)
- `emit_blocking()` - Returns `HookDecision::Allow` or `HookDecision::Deny`, used for `PreToolUse` and `Stop` events

**Plugin Discovery:**
Plugins are discovered from:
1. Project scope: `<project-root>/.agents/plugins/`
2. User scope: `~/.agents/plugins/`
3. Plugin settings enable/disable via: `~/.config/goose/settings.json`, `.config/goose/settings.json`, `.config/goose/settings.local.json`

---

### 2. SESSION STORAGE

**File Locations:**
- **Session manager:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs`
- **Database schema:** Same file, lines 731-837 (SQLite)

**Storage Location:**
- **Base directory:** `~/.config/goose/data/sessions/` (on macOS/Linux) or Windows equivalent (via `etcetera` crate)
- **Database file:** `sessions.db` (SQLite database)
- **Constants:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs` lines 23-24

**Database Schema:**

The sessions are stored in a SQLite database (`sessions.db`) with three main tables:

1. **`sessions` table** (main session record):
   - `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` - Session ID (format: `YYYYMMDD_N` e.g., `20250325_1`)
   - `name TEXT` - User-provided or auto-generated name
   - `user_set_name BOOLEAN` - Whether user set the name
   - `session_type TEXT` - "user", "scheduled", "subagent", "hidden", "terminal", "gateway", or "acp"
   - `working_dir TEXT` - Working directory path
   - `created_at TIMESTAMP`
   - `updated_at TIMESTAMP`
   - `extension_data TEXT` - JSON blob for extension state
   - `total_tokens INTEGER`, `input_tokens INTEGER`, `output_tokens INTEGER` - Last message tokens
   - `accumulated_total_tokens INTEGER`, `accumulated_input_tokens INTEGER`, `accumulated_output_tokens INTEGER` - Running totals
   - `accumulated_cost REAL` - Cumulative cost
   - `schedule_id TEXT` - If scheduled
   - `recipe_json TEXT` - Recipe if used
   - `user_recipe_values_json TEXT` - Recipe parameters
   - `provider_name TEXT` - LLM provider name
   - `model_config_json TEXT` - Model configuration (JSON)
   - `goose_mode TEXT` - "auto" or other mode
   - `archived_at TIMESTAMP` - Archive timestamp if archived
   - `project_id TEXT` - Associated project

2. **`messages` table** (session messages):
   - `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT`
   - `message_id TEXT` - Unique message ID
   - `session_id TEXT REFERENCES sessions(id)`
   - `role TEXT` - "user" or "assistant"
   - `content_json TEXT` - Message content (array of MessageContent objects)
   - `created_timestamp INTEGER` - Unix timestamp
   - `timestamp TIMESTAMP`
   - `tokens INTEGER` - Token count for this message
   - `metadata_json TEXT` - Message metadata (visibility, inference details)

3. **`threads` table** (introduced in schema v10):
   - Organizes sessions into conversation threads

**Session ID Generation:**

Session IDs are generated via SQL in `create_session()` (lines 1231-1274):
```sql
VALUES (
    ? || '_' || CAST(COALESCE((
        SELECT MAX(CAST(SUBSTR(id, 10) AS INTEGER))
        FROM sessions
        WHERE id LIKE ? || '_%'
    ), 0) + 1 AS TEXT)
)
```

Pattern: `YYYYMMDD_N` where:
- `YYYYMMDD` = today's date
- `_N` = incremental counter within the day (1, 2, 3...)

Example: `20250325_1`, `20250325_2`, etc.

### 3. TRANSCRIPT FORMAT / MESSAGE SCHEMA

**File Locations:**
- **Message struct:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose-providers/src/conversation/message.rs`
- **Conversation struct:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose-providers/src/conversation.rs`

**Message Structure** (Rust struct at lines 759-769):

```rust
pub struct Message {
    pub id: Option<String>,           // Unique message ID (generated if None)
    pub role: Role,                   // Role::User or Role::Assistant
    pub created: i64,                 // Unix timestamp
    pub content: Vec<MessageContent>, // Array of content blocks
    pub metadata: MessageMetadata,    // Visibility and inference metadata
}
```

**MessageMetadata** (lines 660-685):

```rust
pub struct MessageMetadata {
    pub user_visible: bool,           // Visible in UI
    pub agent_visible: bool,          // Visible to agent
    pub inference: Option<InferenceMetadata>,
    pub steer: bool,                  // Injected steer flag
}

pub struct InferenceMetadata {
    pub provider: String,             // e.g., "openai"
    pub requested_model: String,      // e.g., "gpt-4o"
    pub resolved_model: Option<String>,  // Actual model if different
}
```

**MessageContent Variants** (lines 262-275):

```rust
pub enum MessageContent {
    Text(TextContent),
    Image(ImageContent),
    ToolRequest(ToolRequest),        // Tool call request
    ToolResponse(ToolResponse),       // Tool execution result
    ToolConfirmationRequest(ToolConfirmationRequest),
    ActionRequired(ActionRequired),
    FrontendToolRequest(FrontendToolRequest),
    Thinking(ThinkingContent),        // Claude thinking/reasoning
    RedactedThinking(RedactedThinkingContent),
    SystemNotification(SystemNotificationContent),
}
```

**ToolRequest Structure** (lines 79-93):

```rust
pub struct ToolRequest {
    pub id: String,
    pub tool_call: ToolResult<CallToolRequestParams>, // Ok or Err
    pub metadata: Option<ProviderMetadata>,
    pub tool_meta: Option<serde_json::Value>,  // Title, chain summary persistence
}
```

**ToolResponse Structure** (lines 174-185):

```rust
pub struct ToolResponse {
    pub id: String,
    pub tool_result: ToolResult<CallToolResult>,  // Tool output
    pub metadata: Option<ProviderMetadata>,
}
```

**Token Usage Data:**

Token information is stored at:
1. **Per-message:** Not in the Message struct itself, but in the `messages` table as `tokens INTEGER` column
2. **Per-session:** In the `sessions` table:
   - `total_tokens`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens` - Last turn
   - `accumulated_total_tokens`, `accumulated_input_tokens`, `accumulated_output_tokens` - Running totals
   - `accumulated_cost REAL` - Cumulative cost

**Conversation Structure** (lines 13-14 in conversation.rs):

```rust
pub struct Conversation(Vec<Message>);
```

Simple wrapper around a vector of messages. Methods include:
- `messages()` - Get all messages
- `agent_visible_messages()` - Filtered by `metadata.agent_visible`
- `user_visible_messages()` - Filtered by `metadata.user_visible`

**JSON Serialization:**

Messages are serialized to JSON when stored in database. Example structure when queried:

```json
{
  "id": "msg_20250325_1_abc123",
  "role": "user",
  "created": 1743148800,
  "content": [
    {
      "type": "text",
      "text": "What does this code do?"
    }
  ],
  "metadata": {
    "userVisible": true,
    "agentVisible": true,
    "inference": null
  }
}
```

### 4. CLI ENTRYPOINTS

**File Location:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose-cli/src/cli.rs`

**Main Commands** (enum at line 781):

**Run a One-Shot Prompt:**
```bash
goose run -t "your prompt here"
goose run --text "your prompt here"
goose run -i instructions.txt
goose run --recipe recipe-name --params key=value
```

Structure from `InputOptions` (lines 169-230):
```rust
pub struct InputOptions {
    pub instructions: Option<String>,    // -i/--instructions FILE
    pub input_text: Option<String>,      // -t/--text TEXT
    pub recipe: Option<String>,          // --recipe RECIPE_NAME
    pub system: Option<String>,          // --system "custom system prompt"
}
```

**Interactive Session Management:**
```bash
goose session                           # Start new session
goose session --resume                  # Resume last session
goose session --resume -n "session-name"  # Resume specific session
goose session --resume --session-id 20250325_1
goose session --resume --fork           # Fork/copy session
goose session --history                 # Show history when resuming
```

Structure from `SessionCommand` / `Identifier` (lines 62-90):
```rust
pub struct Identifier {
    pub name: Option<String>,           // -n/--name
    pub session_id: Option<String>,     // --session-id / --id
    pub path: Option<PathBuf>,          // --path (legacy)
}
```

**Session Identification:**

Sessions are identified by:
1. `--session-id 20250325_1` - Direct ID
2. `--name "session-name"` - Session name (searches in database)
3. No identifier + `--resume` - Uses most recently updated session

**How Session Resumption Works:**

From `get_or_create_session_id()` function (lines ~2020-2080 in cli.rs):
1. If `--resume` flag: looks up session by ID, name, or returns most recent
2. If new session: creates new session with auto-generated ID
3. If `--no-session`: runs headless without persisting session

**Other Commands:**
- `goose acp` - Run as ACP (Agent Client Protocol) agent
- `goose serve` - Start HTTP/WebSocket server
- `goose configure` - Configure settings
- `goose recipe` - Recipe utilities (validate, list, open, deeplink)
- `goose schedule` - Manage scheduled agents
- `goose session list/remove` - Session management

### 5. CONFIGURATION

**File Locations:**
- **Config path module:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/config/paths.rs`
- **Config base:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/config/base.rs`
- **Extensions config:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/config/extensions.rs`
- **Providers config:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/config/providers.rs`

**Config File Locations:**

1. **User config:** `~/.config/goose/config.yaml`
2. **System config:** `/etc/goose/config.yaml` (Linux) or `C:\ProgramData\goose\config.yaml` (Windows)
3. **Additional configs:** Via `GOOSE_ADDITIONAL_CONFIG_FILES` environment variable (split by path separator)
4. **User plugin settings:** `~/.config/goose/settings.json` (enable/disable plugins)
5. **Project plugin settings:** `.config/goose/settings.json` and `.config/goose/settings.local.json`

**Config File Format:**

YAML format (from `config.yaml`). Example structure:

```yaml
extensions:
  developer:
    enabled: true
    stdio: ...
  github:
    enabled: true
    ...

# LLM Provider
openai_api_key: sk-...
anthropic_api_key: sk-...

# Other settings
goose_disable_keyring: false
```

**Key Settings:**

From the Config struct (`/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/config/base.rs` lines 127-148):

- Provider configuration (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, etc.)
- Extension settings (enable/disable, configuration per extension)
- API keys/secrets (stored in system keyring or `~/.config/goose/secrets.yaml`)
- Goose mode (default: "auto")

**Extensions Configuration:**

Extensions are configured in `config.yaml` under the `extensions` key. Example:

```yaml
extensions:
  developer:
    enabled: true
    stdio: "/path/to/stdio"
  github:
    enabled: true
    timeout: 300
```

Each extension entry has:
- `enabled: bool` - Whether active
- Other properties depend on extension type (`Platform`, `Stdio`, `Http`, etc.)

**Hooks are NOT configured in config.yaml** — they are discovered from plugin `hooks.json` files.

---

### 6. PROJECT/WORKING DIRECTORY TRACKING

**File Location:** `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/goose/crates/goose/src/session/session_manager.rs`

**Working Directory Storage:**

The session struct stores:
```rust
pub working_dir: PathBuf,
```

This is stored in the `sessions` table as:
```sql
working_dir TEXT NOT NULL
```

**Database Schema for Directory Queries** (lines 694-824):

Sessions can be filtered by working directory via the `SessionListFilters` struct (lines 304-310):

```rust
pub struct SessionListFilters<'a> {
    pub types: Option<&'a [SessionType]>,
    pub working_dir: Option<&'a Path>,  // Filter by working dir
    pub keyword: Option<&'a str>,
    pub only_sessions_with_messages: bool,
}
```

**Finding Sessions for a Directory:**

To find sessions for a given directory:

```sql
SELECT * FROM sessions
WHERE working_dir = ?
ORDER BY updated_at DESC
```

The `working_dir` is captured when the session is created in `create_session()` (line 1265):

```rust
.bind(&*working_dir.to_string_lossy())
```

It defaults to `std::env::current_dir()` if not explicitly set.

**Project Tracking:**

Additional field:
```rust
pub project_id: Option<String>,
```

Sessions can also have an associated `project_id` for more granular grouping.

The system tracks which directory a session was started in, enabling:
1. Resume sessions in same directory with `--resume`
2. Query all sessions for a directory
3. Organize sessions by project/directory

```
//! Lifecycle hooks support, modelled after the Open Plugins
//! [hooks specification](https://open-plugins.com/agent-builders/components/hooks).
//!
//! Hooks live in `<plugin-root>/hooks/hooks.json` of any plugin discovered by
//! [`crate::plugins::discovery::discover_enabled_plugins`]. The schema is:
//!
//! ```json
//! {
//!   "hooks": {
//!     "PostToolUse": [
//!       {
//!         "matcher": "developer__shell|developer__text_editor",
//!         "hooks": [
//!           { "type": "command", "command": "${PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/log.sh" }
//!         ]
//!       }
//!     ]
//!   }
//! }
//! ```
//!
//! Goose currently supports `type: "command"` actions. Unknown event names and
//! action types are ignored per the spec. Hook scripts receive the JSON event
//! context on stdin and SHOULD exit 0 on success.

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::Stdio;
use std::time::Duration;

use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use regex::Regex;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::Value;
use tokio::io::AsyncWriteExt;
use tokio::process::Command;
use tracing::{debug, info, warn};

use crate::plugins::discovery::{discover_enabled_plugins, DiscoveredPlugin};

/// Default per-hook timeout when the plugin does not specify one.
const DEFAULT_HOOK_TIMEOUT_SECS: u64 = 30;

/// Lifecycle events a hook can subscribe to.
///
/// The variant names match the event names used in `hooks.json`. Unknown
/// events in user config are ignored at load time, per the spec.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum HookEvent {
    PreToolUse,
    PostToolUse,
    PostToolUseFailure,
    SessionStart,
    SessionEnd,
    UserPromptSubmit,
    BeforeReadFile,
    AfterFileEdit,
    BeforeShellExecution,
    AfterShellExecution,
    Stop,
    SubagentStart,
    SubagentStop,
}

impl HookEvent {
    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            HookEvent::PreToolUse => "PreToolUse",
            HookEvent::PostToolUse => "PostToolUse",
            HookEvent::PostToolUseFailure => "PostToolUseFailure",
            HookEvent::SessionStart => "SessionStart",
            HookEvent::SessionEnd => "SessionEnd",
            HookEvent::UserPromptSubmit => "UserPromptSubmit",
            HookEvent::BeforeReadFile => "BeforeReadFile",
            HookEvent::AfterFileEdit => "AfterFileEdit",
            HookEvent::BeforeShellExecution => "BeforeShellExecution",
            HookEvent::AfterShellExecution => "AfterShellExecution",
            HookEvent::Stop => "Stop",
            HookEvent::SubagentStart => "SubagentStart",
            HookEvent::SubagentStop => "SubagentStop",
        }
    }

    fn from_name(name: &str) -> Option<Self> {
        Some(match name {
            "PreToolUse" => HookEvent::PreToolUse,
            "PostToolUse" => HookEvent::PostToolUse,
            "PostToolUseFailure" => HookEvent::PostToolUseFailure,
            "SessionStart" => HookEvent::SessionStart,
            "SessionEnd" => HookEvent::SessionEnd,
            "UserPromptSubmit" => HookEvent::UserPromptSubmit,
            "BeforeReadFile" => HookEvent::BeforeReadFile,
            "AfterFileEdit" => HookEvent::AfterFileEdit,
            "BeforeShellExecution" => HookEvent::BeforeShellExecution,
            "AfterShellExecution" => HookEvent::AfterShellExecution,
            "Stop" => HookEvent::Stop,
            "SubagentStart" => HookEvent::SubagentStart,
            "SubagentStop" => HookEvent::SubagentStop,
            _ => return None,
        })
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for HookEvent {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.name())
    }
}

/// Top-level `hooks.json` shape.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Deserialize)]
struct HooksFile {
    #[serde(default)]
    hooks: HashMap<String, Vec<RawHookRule>>,
}

/// One rule within a `hooks.json` event entry.
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
struct RawHookRule {
    #[serde(default)]
    matcher: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    hooks: Vec<RawHookAction>,
}

/// One action entry under a rule's `hooks` array. We only run `command`
/// today, but we deserialize the others so that loading a plugin which uses
/// them does not fail.
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
struct RawHookAction {
    #[serde(default, rename = "type")]
    action_type: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    command: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    timeout: Option<u64>,
}

/// A loaded, plugin-bound hook rule ready to execute.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
struct LoadedRule {
    plugin_name: String,
    plugin_root: PathBuf,
    matcher: Option<Regex>,
    actions: Vec<LoadedAction>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
enum LoadedAction {
    Command { command: String, timeout: Duration },
}

/// Context passed to a hook as JSON on stdin.
///
/// The `matcher_context` is the string the rule's `matcher` regex is tested
/// against — tool name for tool events, file path for file events, command
/// string for shell events. Other fields carry the same value plus the
/// raw JSON payload of the underlying event so scripts can do richer things
/// without needing to parse a hook-specific schema.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]
pub struct HookContext {
    pub event: String,
    pub session_id: String,
    pub matcher_context: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub tool_name: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub tool_input: Option<Value>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub tool_output: Option<Value>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub message: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub working_dir: Option<String>,
}

impl HookContext {
    pub fn new(event: HookEvent, session_id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            event: event.to_string(),
            session_id: session_id.into(),
            matcher_context: None,
            tool_name: None,
            tool_input: None,
            tool_output: None,
            message: None,
            working_dir: None,
        }
    }

    pub fn with_tool(mut self, tool_name: impl Into<String>, tool_input: Option<Value>) -> Self {
        let name = tool_name.into();
        self.matcher_context = Some(name.clone());
        self.tool_name = Some(name);
        self.tool_input = tool_input;
        self
    }

    pub fn with_tool_output(mut self, output: Value) -> Self {
        self.tool_output = Some(output);
```

use etcetera::{choose_app_strategy, AppStrategy, AppStrategyArgs};
use std::path::PathBuf;

pub struct Paths;

impl Paths {
    fn get_dir(dir_type: DirType) -> PathBuf {
        if let Ok(test_root) = std::env::var("GOOSE_PATH_ROOT") {
            let base = PathBuf::from(test_root);
            match dir_type {
                DirType::Config => base.join("config"),
                DirType::Data => base.join("data"),
                DirType::State => base.join("state"),
                DirType::Plugins => base.join(".agents").join("plugins"),
                DirType::Agents => base.join(".agents").join("agents"),
            }
        } else {
            // NOTE: "Block" is kept here for backwards compatibility with existing
            // user config/data directories (e.g. ~/Library/Application Support/Block/goose/).
            // Changing this would orphan existing installations.
            let strategy = choose_app_strategy(AppStrategyArgs {
                top_level_domain: "Block".to_string(),
                author: "Block".to_string(),
                app_name: "goose".to_string(),
            })
            .expect("goose requires a home dir");

            match dir_type {
                DirType::Config => strategy.config_dir(),
                DirType::Data => strategy.data_dir(),
                DirType::State => strategy.state_dir().unwrap_or(strategy.data_dir()),
                DirType::Plugins => strategy.home_dir().join(".agents").join("plugins"),
                DirType::Agents => strategy.home_dir().join(".agents").join("agents"),
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn config_dir() -> PathBuf {
        Self::get_dir(DirType::Config)
    }

    pub fn data_dir() -> PathBuf {
        Self::get_dir(DirType::Data)
    }

    pub fn state_dir() -> PathBuf {
        Self::get_dir(DirType::State)
    }

    pub fn plugins_dir() -> PathBuf {
        Self::get_dir(DirType::Plugins)
    }

    pub fn agents_dir() -> PathBuf {
        Self::get_dir(DirType::Agents)
    }

    pub fn in_state_dir(subpath: &str) -> PathBuf {
        Self::state_dir().join(subpath)
    }

    pub fn in_config_dir(subpath: &str) -> PathBuf {
        Self::config_dir().join(subpath)
    }

    pub fn in_data_dir(subpath: &str) -> PathBuf {
        Self::data_dir().join(subpath)
    }
}

enum DirType {
    Config,
    Data,
    State,
    Plugins,
    Agents,
}

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use serde::Deserialize;

use crate::plugins::plugin_install_dir;

/// A plugin found on disk and not disabled by any settings file.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct DiscoveredPlugin {
    pub name: String,
    pub root: PathBuf,
    pub scope: PluginScope,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PluginScope {
    User,
    Project,
}

/// Settings file format from <https://open-plugins.com/plugin-builders/installation>.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Deserialize)]
struct PluginSettings {
    #[serde(default, rename = "enabledPlugins")]
    enabled: Vec<String>,
    #[serde(default, rename = "disabledPlugins")]
    disabled: Vec<String>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
enum SettingsScope {
    Local,
    Project,
    User,
}

/// Discover all plugins that should be considered active.
///
/// `project_root`, when supplied, enables project + local scope settings and
/// project-scope `.agents/plugins/` lookups.
pub fn discover_enabled_plugins(project_root: Option<&Path>) -> Vec<DiscoveredPlugin> {
    let scoped_settings = load_all_settings(project_root);
    let mut found: HashMap<String, DiscoveredPlugin> = HashMap::new();

    if let Some(root) = project_root {
        for (name, root) in list_dir_children(&project_plugin_dir(root)) {
            found.entry(name.clone()).or_insert(DiscoveredPlugin {
                name,
                root,
                scope: PluginScope::Project,
            });
        }
    }
    for (name, root) in list_dir_children(&plugin_install_dir()) {
        found.entry(name.clone()).or_insert(DiscoveredPlugin {
            name,
            root,
            scope: PluginScope::User,
        });
    }

    found
        .into_values()
        .filter(|plugin| is_enabled(&plugin.name, &scoped_settings))
        .collect()
}

fn is_enabled(plugin_name: &str, scoped_settings: &[(SettingsScope, PluginSettings)]) -> bool {
    for scope in [
        SettingsScope::Local,
        SettingsScope::Project,
        SettingsScope::User,
    ] {
        let Some(settings) = scoped_settings
            .iter()
            .find_map(|(s, settings)| (*s == scope).then_some(settings))
        else {
            continue;
        };

        let listed_disabled = settings.disabled.iter().any(|n| n == plugin_name);
        let listed_enabled = settings.enabled.iter().any(|n| n == plugin_name);

        if listed_disabled {
            return false;
        }
        if listed_enabled {
            return true;
        }
    }

    true
}

fn project_plugin_dir(project_root: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    project_root.join(".agents").join("plugins")
}

fn list_dir_children(dir: &Path) -> Vec<(String, PathBuf)> {
    let entries = match std::fs::read_dir(dir) {
        Ok(e) => e,
        Err(_) => return Vec::new(),
    };
    entries
        .flatten()
        .filter_map(|entry| {
            let path = entry.path();
            if !path.is_dir() {
                return None;
            }
            let name = path.file_name()?.to_str()?.to_string();
            Some((name, path))
        })
        .collect()
}

fn load_all_settings(project_root: Option<&Path>) -> Vec<(SettingsScope, PluginSettings)> {
    let mut paths: Vec<(SettingsScope, PathBuf)> = Vec::new();
    if let Some(path) = user_settings_path() {
```

    /// Process a single message and exit
    pub async fn headless(&mut self, prompt: String) -> Result<()> {
        self.agent
            .emit_hook(goose::hooks::HookEvent::SessionStart, &self.session_id)
            .await;
        let message = Message::user().with_text(&prompt);
        let result = self
            .process_message(message, CancellationToken::default(), false)
            .await;
        self.agent
            .emit_hook(goose::hooks::HookEvent::SessionEnd, &self.session_id)
            .await;
        result?;
        Ok(())
    }
