```
import { type Kysely, sql } from "kysely"

// Drops the hand-written repo_checkpoints.agent column and replaces it with
// primary_agent_raw — a stored generated column projected from sessions[].
// Steps stay independent so the migration is idempotent on re-run: each ALTER
// swallows the "already done" error for its own step.

// Matches MAX_SESSIONS_PER_CHECKPOINT in api/src/lib/constants.ts. Bounds how
// many session slots the COALESCE chain has to consider.
const MAX_SESSIONS = 50

// One COALESCE arm per session slot. JSON_EXTRACT on a missing index returns
// SQL NULL; JSON_UNQUOTE preserves that. The NULLIF chain skips the literal
// strings that all mean "no agent identified" (the JSON null value comes back
// from JSON_UNQUOTE as the four-character string "null"). The chain walks
// back-to-front so the most recent named agent wins, matching the TS
// getCheckpointPrimaryAgent helper.
function buildSlotExpr(idx: number): string {
  return [
    "NULLIF(",
    "  NULLIF(",
    "    NULLIF(",
    `      NULLIF(JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(sessions, '$[${idx}].agent')), 'Unknown'),`,
    "    'unknown'),",
    "  'null'),",
    "'')",
  ].join("\n")
}

function buildGeneratedExpr(): string {
  const arms: string[] = []
  for (let i = MAX_SESSIONS - 1; i >= 0; i--) arms.push(buildSlotExpr(i))
  arms.push("'Unknown'")
  return `COALESCE(\n${arms.join(",\n")}\n)`
}

async function runIgnoring(db: Kysely<unknown>, ddl: string, ignore: string[]) {
  try {
    await sql.raw(ddl).execute(db)
  } catch (e: unknown) {
    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : ""
    if (ignore.some((needle) => msg.includes(needle))) return
    throw e
  }
}

export async function up(db: Kysely<unknown>): Promise<void> {
  await runIgnoring(db, "ALTER TABLE repo_checkpoints DROP COLUMN agent", [
    "check that column/key exists",
    "doesn't exist",
  ])
  await runIgnoring(
    db,
    `ALTER TABLE repo_checkpoints
       ADD COLUMN primary_agent_raw VARCHAR(64)
       GENERATED ALWAYS AS (${buildGeneratedExpr()}) STORED NOT NULL`,
    ["Duplicate column name", "already exists"],
  )
  await runIgnoring(
    db,
```

> AGENT

No migration needed — the generated column stores the *raw* agent string (it only filters "Unknown"/"null"/empty), and normalization to a known agent happens in TypeScript, which now knows Goose. 

Done — here's what `entire.io` got, on branch **`goose-agent-support`** (commit `2c2d9a3728`, 10 files, +486):

**Transcript rendering** — `api/src/lib/transcript-parsers/goose-parser.ts`: parses Goose's stored export format (the JSON document `entire-agent-goose` materializes) into the app's message model — user prompts, assistant turns merged across consecutive messages, tool calls with their `toolResponse` results folded in, error statuses, model extraction (`anthropic/claude-opus-4.6`), tool classification (shell/fileOps/search/agent), and `load_skill` skill attribution. Verified against your real checkpoint transcript: 2 rendered messages, the assistant turn carrying all 9 tool calls.

**Agent attribution** — "goose" added to the API and frontend agent registries with label **Goose**, a goose-silhouette SVG avatar, and lime color classes (light + dark). Checkpoint metadata already records `agent: "Goose"` (from the binary's `info`), which now normalizes to the goose identity instead of falling back to the gray "Unknown".

**Validation**: all 258 transcript-parser tests pass (including new goose + registry cases), `tsc --noEmit` clean in both `api/` and `frontend/`, oxlint clean.

A few notes:

- **Previously-synced checkpoints should self-heal**: the API tracks transcripts that were parsed with the fallback parser and re-parses them once a dedicated parser exists (`hasNonFallbackParser`) — your existing synced sessions should pick up proper rendering without re-ingestion.
- **Skipped intentionally**: `website/src/lib/agents.ts` (marketing site doesn't list Kiro either — seems curated) and the planetscale `primary_agent_raw` test (needs the local MySQL, but I confirmed the SQL is agent-agnostic).
- **lefthook wasn't on PATH**, so pre-commit hooks didn't run on my commit — worth a `pnpm lint`/format pass per repo convention before pushing.
- The goose silhouette SVG is hand-drawn placeholder art — if Block's goose project has brand assets you're allowed to use, swap `frontend/public/images/logos/agents/goose.svg`.

That completes the full chain: **goose → entire-agent-goose** (captures + compacts), **entire CLI** (renders `--full` locally), **entire.io** (renders + attributes in the web UI) — three branches ready for PRs: `goose-agent` (external-agents), `external-agent-transcript-display` (cli), `goose-agent-support` (entire.io).

# Entire

A full-stack application with GitHub authentication and a React frontend.

## Tech Stack

**Frontend:**

- React 19 with TypeScript
- Vite 7
- TailwindCSS 4
- TanStack Router, Query, and Table
- Storybook for component development
- Vitest for testing

**API:**

- Cloudflare Workers with Hono
- Planetscale (MySQL) database
- Cloudflare KV for session/cache storage
- GitHub OAuth authentication

## Getting Started

```bash
brew install 1password-cli jq
```

For local MySQL, install a Docker-compatible runtime. On macOS we recommend Colima; Docker Desktop and OrbStack also work.

```bash
brew install colima docker docker-compose
colima start
```

```bash
pnpm install
mise run secrets
```

See [docs/setup.md](docs/setup.md) for full setup (MySQL, Hyperdrive, manual config without 1Password, and container runtimes).

When setting up a worktree, `scripts/setup --copy-mysql-store` can copy the main checkout's local MySQL store. If source MySQL is running, setup prompts before stopping it; default is No.

For development commands and conventions see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) (also [api/CLAUDE.md](api/CLAUDE.md) and [frontend/CLAUDE.md](frontend/CLAUDE.md)).

## Project Structure

```
entire.io/
├── api/                    # Cloudflare Workers API
│   ├── src/
│   │   ├── index.ts        # Main entry point
│   │   ├── routes/         # API routes
│   │   ├── lib/            # Utilities
│   │   └── types/          # TypeScript types
│   ├── db/
│   │   ├── migrations/     # Kysely migrations — create with `pnpm run db:create-migration <name>`
│   │   └── types.ts        # Database types (auto-generated)
│   ├── scripts/            # CLI scripts (migrate, reset, deploy)
```

Your `entire.io` checkout has never run the full local stack (colima stopped, only `.dev.vars.template` — no secrets), so there are two tiers depending on how much you want to see:

## Quick proof in 10 seconds (no backend)

Run the new parser directly against your real checkpoint transcript:

```bash
cd ~/Documents/work/entire.io/api
npx tsx -e "
import { GooseParser } from './src/lib/transcript-parsers/goose-parser'
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
const r = new GooseParser().parse(readFileSync('/Users/goose-guest/Documents/agent-experiments/test-goose-entire/.entire/metadata/20260611_36/full.jsonl','utf8'))
for (const m of r.messages) console.log(m.role.toUpperCase(), '—', m.content.slice(0,100), m.toolCalls?.map(t=>t.name) ?? '')
"
```

That shows exactly what the web UI will render: your prompt, then the assistant turn with text + all 9 tool calls.

## See it in the actual app

You need the standard local dev setup from `docs/setup.md` — this part I can't do for you since `mise run secrets` needs your 1Password auth:

```bash
brew install colima docker docker-compose 1password-cli jq   # if not installed
colima start
cd ~/Documents/work/entire.io     # on branch goose-agent-support
pnpm install
mise run secrets                  # pulls .dev.vars via 1Password (interactive)
mise run api-db-start && mise run api-db-migrate
mise run dev                      # api :8787, frontend :5173, search :8686
```

Then in the app at `http://localhost:5173`:

1. Sign in with GitHub and connect a repo.
2. **Your goose checkpoints have to reach the app via GitHub** — push `test-goose-entire` to GitHub including Entire's metadata ref (`git push origin main entire/checkpoints/v1`), and connect that repo.
3. Open the synced session — you should see the conversation rendered (prompts + responses + tool calls) and the session attributed to **Goose** with the lime avatar instead of the gray "Unknown".

If you already have a working dev env in another checkout/worktree, the faster route is just `git fetch` + check out `goose-agent-support` there and restart `mise run dev`.

One thing to know: checkpoints that synced **before** this branch (parsed with the fallback parser) get re-parsed automatically once the goose parser exists — that's built into the API — so your earlier sessions should render without re-pushing anything.

> AGENT

Short answer: you need *some* way to fill `api/.dev.vars`, and 1Password is just the fastest one for you. Looking at the template, here's what actually matters:

**The only secrets you can't make up** are the GitHub App credentials (`GITHUB_CLIENT_ID`, `GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GITHUB_APP_NAME`, `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`, `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) — that's what powers "Sign in with GitHub" and the repo sync that pulls your checkpoints in. Without those, you can't get data into the app at all.

**Everything else is dummy-able or optional** for what you're trying to see:
- `ENCRYPTION_KEY` / `SESSION_SECRET` / `ENTIRE_WEB_API_TOKEN` — any random strings work locally
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_ANALYSIS` — only powers the AI analysis pipeline; transcript rendering doesn't need it
- Turbopuffer/OpenAI/Cohere/PostHog — search and analytics, skippable
- `frontend/.env` — the PostHog values can be blank/dummy; only `VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8787` matters

So your two options:

1. **`! op signin` then `! mise run secrets`** — since you're on the entire.io team, the shared dev GitHub App lives in the Shared vault and this takes 30 seconds. This is the intended path.
2. **No 1Password**: copy `api/.dev.vars.template` → `api/.dev.vars`, fill the dummy-able values by hand, and create your **own GitHub App** (callback `http://localhost:8787/...`, generate a private key) for the five GitHub fields. That's 15–20 minutes of GitHub UI fiddling to avoid one `op signin`.

Given your work email, option 1 is really the answer unless there's a reason you can't access the Shared vault. Type `! op signin` in the prompt and I'll keep going from there.

```
import { Hono } from "hono"

import { isLocalDev, type Env, type Variables } from "../env"
import { getDb } from "../lib/db"
import { requireAuth } from "../lib/middleware"

export const authDevApiRoutes = new Hono<{
  Bindings: Env
  Variables: Variables
}>()

authDevApiRoutes.use("*", requireAuth)

authDevApiRoutes.post("/bootstrap-admin", async (c) => {
  if (!isLocalDev(c.env)) {
    return c.notFound()
  }

  const userId = c.get("userId")
  const db = getDb(c.env)
  const user = await db.users.findById(userId)

  if (!user) {
    return c.json({ error: "User not found" }, 404)
  }

  if (!user.is_admin) {
    await db.users.setAdmin(userId, true)
    await c.env.ENTIRE_CACHE.delete(`user:${userId}`)
  }

  return c.json({ is_admin: true })
})
```

**To run the full web app: yes, you need the secrets** — specifically the five GitHub App values. The rest can be garbage strings. There's no anonymous mode, no seed-data script, no login bypass.

Your realistic paths, in order of effort:

1. **`! op signin`** (~30 seconds) — you're on the team, the Shared vault has everything, `mise run secrets` does the rest. This is what the repo expects, and everything else (colima, MySQL, migrations) is already done and waiting.
2. **Hand-roll `.dev.vars` with your own GitHub App** (~20 min) — works, but you'd create a personal GitHub App with a localhost callback just to avoid one `op signin`.
3. **Don't run the app at all** — if the goal is confidence the code works, that's already established: the parser is proven against your real checkpoint transcript (the 10-second `npx tsx` command from earlier shows exactly what the UI will render), 258 parser tests pass, and typecheck is clean. The PR can go up on that evidence, and you'd see it rendered on the deployed preview/staging environment once it merges — no local stack needed.

Honestly: if you have 1Password access, option 1. If `op signin` fails because you don't have the Shared vault, tell me and we'll do option 2 — I can script everything except the GitHub UI clicks.

> AGENT

Yes — completely real. Your goose sessions have created **three actual checkpoints**, stored as git commits on a dedicated orphan branch (`entire/checkpoints/v1`) in your test repo:

```
9602158 Update summary for checkpoint 7c4d789dd1c6 (session: 20260611_37)  ← your "custom daily goal" session
6023012 Checkpoint: 7c4d789dd1c6
150228b Update summary for checkpoint 2bbb794089bb (session: 20260611_36)  ← the water tracker session
14e7a4e Checkpoint: 2bbb794089bb
3affe83 Checkpoint: a3e661c62137                                           ← the first "hello marvin" readme
```

Each checkpoint is a content-addressed directory on that branch containing:

- `full.jsonl` — the complete goose transcript (this is what the parser I wrote renders)
- `metadata.json` — session info: agent ("Goose"), session ID, token usage, files touched
- `prompt.txt` — your prompt
- `content_hash.txt` — integrity hash

And they're linked to your regular commits: `e1eb1b0 water tracking app` and `1c3c91e added custom daily goal` on `main` carry `Entire-Checkpoint:` trailers pointing at these IDs. That's the whole design — your code history stays clean on `main`, while the AI context lives on the metadata branch, and the trailer ties them together.

So everything is genuinely persisted as git data. It's also why the web app step works the way it does: pushing `entire/checkpoints/v1` to GitHub uploads these exact commits, and entire.io reads `full.jsonl` + `metadata.json` from them — which is precisely what the goose parser and attribution I added consume. (Also notable: I see a third session, `20260611_37`, with a generated summary — so checkpointing, commit linking, and summarization have all been working across your real usage, not just my tests.)

> DEVELOPER

no i mean in the UI

> AGENT

Ah — no, the UI never *creates* checkpoints; it mirrors the ones your CLI already made. When your repo syncs, those three checkpoints we just looked at become first-class objects in the web app. Concretely, you'll see:

- **On commits**: `e1eb1b0 water tracking app` and `1c3c91e added custom daily goal` show up annotated with their checkpoints (via the `Entire-Checkpoint:` trailers) — checkpoint ID, prompt, files touched, token usage.
- **Sessions list**: sessions `20260611_36` and `20260611_37` appear as entries, each attributed to **Goose** (lime avatar + "Goose" label, thanks to the registry change — previously they'd have shown as gray "Unknown").
- **Session/checkpoint detail page**: the conversation timeline rendered from `full.jsonl` — your prompts, Goose's responses, and each tool call (`write`, `shell`, `tree`, `load_skill`…) with inputs and outputs, via the new parser. Plus the AI summaries that were already generated ("water tracking app" etc.).
- **Recap/contribution views**: Goose counts as its own agent in the per-agent breakdowns rather than lumping into Unknown.

So the pipeline is: **CLI creates** checkpoints locally (done — they exist in your repo) → **GitHub push** of `main` + `entire/checkpoints/v1` uploads them → **app syncs and displays**. Nothing new is generated app-side except indexing/analysis on top of what's already in git.

Which means once you finish the `op signin` → `mise run secrets` → `mise run dev` → connect-repo dance, those three existing checkpoints from your earlier goose sessions are exactly what will appear — no need to run goose again to have something to look at.

**Yes — with one precise caveat.** I just ran your *actual committed checkpoint* (pulled from `entire/checkpoints/v1` in your repo) through the exact code paths entire.io uses, with the changes:

```
attribution: "Goose" → goose → "Goose"            ← was: → unknown → gray "Unknown"
dedicated parser registered: true                  ← was: false → FallbackParser
parser: Goose | model: anthropic/claude-opus-4.6
parsed messages: 2 | fallback: false | warning: none
first user: "in our readme that we made that says hello marvin..."
assistant tools: shell, tree, write, load_skill, write, write, write, shell, tree
```

That's the whole server-side pipeline the UI sits on top of: the raw `"agent": "Goose"` string from your checkpoint metadata resolves to the goose identity (which drives the avatar, label, and color), and the stored transcript parses into the message/tool-call structure with zero fallback. Before our changes, that same input produced "Unknown" attribution and an unparsed transcript.

The caveat: the React components that paint this — `SessionTimelineView`, `AgentAvatar` — I haven't seen render in a browser, because that needs the running app (the secrets blocker). But those components are agent-agnostic: they consume exactly the two outputs verified above (`TranscriptMessage[]` and an `AgentId`), and they're the same components already rendering Claude/Amp/Kiro sessions in production every day. The only agent-specific things they touch are the registry entry, logo file, and CSS class we added.

So: parsing ✅ proven on your real data, attribution ✅ proven on your real data, pixels — extremely likely but unverified until `mise run dev` comes up or this hits a preview deploy. The one visual I'd actually double-check on a live render is my hand-drawn goose SVG looking decent at avatar size.

```
Perfect. Now let me create a comprehensive summary of my findings:

## Summary

Based on my thorough investigation of the trails feature and its relationship with agents, here's what I found:

### 1. **What are Trails? (Data Model)**

Trails are PR-like workflow objects stored in the database with the following structure:

**Database Schema (`/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/api/db/schema.ts`):**
- `RepoTrails` table: Stores trail metadata (id, number, branch, status, title, body_editor_document_id, etc.)
- `RepoSessions` table: Stores individual CLI sessions with `agent: string | null` field
- `RepoCheckpoints` table: Aggregates multiple sessions, includes `session_ids` and `sessions` JSON columns
- `SandboxRuns` table: Stores automated runner executions with `agent: string` field
- `RepoTrailMonitorResults`: Stores evaluation metrics from trail runners

**Relationship to Sessions/Checkpoints:**
- Sessions are individual CLI invocations recorded via the Goose CLI (or any agent's CLI)
- Checkpoints aggregate multiple sessions and commits
- Trails display checkpoints grouped by branch and commit, extracting unique agent strings from session data
- Trails also support automated sandbox runs that launch agents in isolated environments (currently used for "fix_ci" and trail evaluations)

### 2. **Where Trails Reference Agents**

**Agent references in trails code paths:**

| File Path | Usage | Type |
|-----------|-------|------|
| `/api/src/lib/agents.ts` | AGENT_IDS, AGENT_LABELS, getAgentId, getAgentLabel | Registry (already has "goose") |
| `/api/src/lib/checkpoint-mapper.ts` | Extracts agents from session data: `agents = [...new Set(sessions.map((s) => s.agent))]` | Display mapper |
| `/api/src/routes/trails.ts` | Calls `mapCheckpointToTrailSummary()` which extracts agents | Trail detail endpoint |
| `/api/src/lib/trail-runner-launcher.ts` | Reads `runnerConfig.agent` for sandbox execution | Runner launcher (specific agents) |
| `/api/src/lib/agents/trail-eval.ts` | TRAIL_EVAL_AGENT_IDS for metric evaluation | Trail evaluation metrics |
| `/frontend/src/lib/agents.ts` | agents Record<AgentId, AgentConfig> (already has "goose") | Frontend registry |
| `/frontend/src/domains/platform/trails/components/TrailTimeline.tsx` | Collects agents array from checkpoints for display | Timeline display |

**Key insight:** Trails display sessions through the **shared agent registry** (which you already updated). They don't have their own hardcoded agent lists. The agents field in `TrailCheckpointSummary` is dynamically extracted from session data at display time.

### 3. **Trail Runs / Runners / Sandbox Runs**

**Purpose:** Automated execution of agents in isolated sandboxes

**Who uses it:**
- `trail-runner-launcher.ts` - Launches configured runners on trail branches (fix_ci, trail-summary, trail evaluations)
- `configured-runner-launcher.ts` - Generic v2 runner executor
- Currently only "claude" is hardcoded in test configs and some runner v2 configs

**Critical finding:** This is **opt-in per-repository via runner configs**. Goose sessions recorded via CLI **do not interact with sandbox runs** — they're just recorded as checkpoint sessions. Sandbox runs are only spawned when:
1. A `.entire/runners/v2/` config explicitly requests it
2. Trail CI is enabled and a runner config is present
3. Evaluations are triggered via the web UI

Goose CLI sessions will display fine in trails without any sandbox run support.

### 4. **Other Agent References to Check**

**Search results:**
- `/api/src/routes/recap.ts` - Per-agent recap aggregates (uses shared registry, already supports goose)
- `/api/src/lib/activity-aggregator.ts` - Record<AgentId, number> maps (uses shared registry)
- `/api/src/lib/checkpoint-agent-attribution.ts` - getCheckpointPrimaryAgent() (uses shared registry)
- Database schema: All agent fields are `string | null` or `string` (no enums, no hardcoded validation)

**No hardcoded agent lists found in:**
- SQL configs or migrations
- Trails display code
- Session/checkpoint storage
- Analysis pipelines
- Recap/contribution charts (all use getAgentId/getAgentLabel from shared registry)

### **Verdict: NO trails-specific changes needed for goose**

**Why:**
1. ✅ Goose is already in `/api/src/lib/agents.ts` AGENT_IDS, AGENT_LABELS
2. ✅ Goose is already in `/frontend/src/lib/agents.ts` agents registry  
3. ✅ Goose parser is already registered in `/api/src/lib/transcript-parsers/registry.ts`
4. ✅ All trails code paths use the shared agent registry (no hardcoded lists)
5. ✅ Sessions store agent as a plain string field (no enum validation)
6. ✅ Checkpoints extract agents dynamically from session data
7. ✅ Trail timeline collects and passes agents array to frontend
8. ✅ Frontend renders agents via shared getAgent() registry lookup

**Sessions/checkpoints recorded via Goose CLI will display correctly in trails immediately** — no code changes required. The agent label "Goose" will appear in recaps, contribution charts, and trail activity summaries.

**File Paths Reference:**
- `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/api/src/lib/agents.ts`
- `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/frontend/src/lib/agents.ts`
- `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/api/src/lib/transcript-parsers/registry.ts`
- `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/api/src/lib/checkpoint-mapper.ts`
- `/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/entire.io/api/src/routes/trails.ts`
```

> AGENT

**No — trails need nothing extra.** I had a sub-agent sweep the whole trails surface and then double-checked the one suspicious list myself. Here's why you're covered:

**Trails are display-layer for agent data, not agent-specific.** A trail is a PR-like workflow object that groups checkpoints/sessions by branch. Everywhere it touches agents, it flows through the shared registry we already updated:

- `checkpoint-mapper.ts` extracts agents dynamically from session JSON (`sessions.map(s => s.agent)`) — your `"Goose"` string passes straight through
- `TrailTimeline.tsx` and the trail detail page render those via `getAgent()` → the goose entry with avatar/label/color
- Recap, activity aggregator, and contribution charts all build their `Record<AgentId, number>` maps from `AGENT_IDS` — goose is in that list now, and typecheck passing proves no exhaustive map was missed
- DB columns store agent as a plain `string` — no enums, no SQL agent lists

**The two things that *look* agent-specific but aren't relevant:**

1. `TRAIL_EVAL_AGENT_IDS` — I checked this directly: those "agents" are `trail-confidence`, `trail-risk`, `trail-drift` — evaluation *runner* identities for trail metrics, a different namespace entirely from coding agents. Nothing to add.
2. **Sandbox runs / runner launchers** — these *launch* agents in cloud sandboxes (fix-CI, evaluations), opt-in per repo via `.entire/runners/v2/` configs, and currently Claude-centric. Goose sessions recorded via your CLI never touch this path — it's only relevant if someday you wanted entire.io to *spawn* goose in a sandbox, which would be a separate (much bigger) project.

So the full inventory of what a new display-only agent needs in `entire.io` is exactly what's already on the `goose-agent-support` branch: the two registry entries, the transcript parser, the logo, and the CSS class. Trails, recaps, search, and contribution views all inherit it from there.

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"Add entire-agent-goose: Goose external agent\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What\n\nAdds `entire-agent-goose`, an external agent binary integrating [Goose](https://github.com/block/goose) (Block's open-source coding agent) with Entire — built via the repo's `/entire-external-agent` skill (research → write-tests → implement).\n\n**Capabilities**: `hooks`, `transcript_analyzer`, `transcript_preparer`, `token_calculator`, `compact_transcript`\n\n- **Hooks** — `install-hooks` writes a project-scope goose plugin (`.agents/plugins/entire/hooks/hooks.json`) forwarding goose's native `SessionStart` / `UserPromptSubmit` / `Stop` / `SessionEnd` lifecycle events to `entire hooks goose <verb>`. Goose discovers project plugins from its working directory; no global goose config is touched.\n- **Transcripts** — goose stores sessions in SQLite with no per-session file; `prepare-transcript` (and best-effort on stop/session-end) materializes one via `goose session export --format json` next to goose's sessions.db. Transcript positions are message indexes since every export rewrites the document.\n- **Tokens** — from the export's accumulated session totals (per-message counts are NULL in goose's DB; no cache split — documented in AGENT.md).\n- **Compact transcripts** — converts the export to Entire Transcript Format so conversations render in `entire explain --full` and the web UI.\n\nAll research is verified against real goose 1.37.0 hook captures and exports (see `AGENT.md` + `scripts/verify-goose.sh`, which probes via a throwaway workspace without touching global config).\n\n## Design note worth maintainer eyes 👀\n\nThe session dir is goose's data dir (`~/.local/share/goose/sessions`), deliberately **outside the repo**: Entire attributes a session to the first registered agent whose `get-session-dir` contains the transcript path, and a repo-local dir like `.entire/tmp/goose` nests inside amp's/kiro's `.entire/tmp` — with `entire-agent-amp` installed, Amp silently claimed every goose session and TurnEnd checkpoints were skipped. Since **amp and kiro share `.entire/tmp` today**, the same misattribution risk exists between them; the CLI may want longest-prefix matching in `AgentForTranscriptPath`.\n\n## Shared harness fixes (benefit all agents)\n\n- `e2e/entire/entire.go`: `RewindList` tolerates the deprecation banner newer entire versions print before the JSON\n- `e2e/lifecycle_test.go`: `SessionPersistence` resolves the session dir via the agent's `get-session-dir` instead of hardcoding `.entire/tmp`, and only counts files fresher than the test start\n\n## Testing\n\n- Protocol compliance (`external-agents-tests verify`): **29/29 pass**\n- Lifecycle e2e (`E2E_AGENT=goose`, real goose + real provider): **all 8 scenarios pass** (enable, hooks install, single/multi prompt checkpoints, session persistence, rewind pre/post-commit, interactive tmux session)\n- Unit tests: hook parsing, install/uninstall round-trip, transcript analysis, token calc, compact conversion — all against payloads captured from real goose sessions\n\n## Companion PRs\n\n- entirehq/entire.io#2354 — web rendering + attribution for goose\n- entireio/cli (branch ready) — `--full`/`--verbose` external-agent transcript display fix\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -2",
  "description": "Open PR for entire-agent-goose"
}
```

```
func (a *Agent) ExtractModifiedFiles(path string, offset int) ([]string, int, error) {
	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, 0, err
	}
	export, err := parseGooseExport(data)
	if err != nil {
		return []string{}, 0, nil
	}
	return modifiedFilesFromExport(export, offset), len(export.Conversation), nil
}

func (a *Agent) ExtractPrompts(sessionRef string, offset int) ([]string, error) {
	data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	export, err := parseGooseExport(data)
	if err != nil {
		return []string{}, nil
	}
	prompts := []string{}
	for _, msg := range messagesFromOffset(export, offset) {
		if msg.Role != "user" {
			continue
		}
		for _, content := range msg.Content {
			if content.Type == "text" && content.Text != "" {
				prompts = append(prompts, content.Text)
			}
		}
	}
	return prompts, nil
}

// ExtractSummary returns goose's session name, which goose auto-generates
// from the conversation.
func (a *Agent) ExtractSummary(sessionRef string) (string, bool, error) {
	data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef)
	if err != nil {
		return "", false, err
	}
	export, err := parseGooseExport(data)
	if err != nil || export.ID == "" || export.Name == "" {
		return "", false, nil
	}
	return export.Name, true, nil
```
